Request: OWN-5

Question: How are Sanofi's vaccine sales likely to develop in the 2026/27 and 2027/28 seasons, specifically Beyfortus US orders through the CDC and private channels against Merck's Enflonsia and maternal RSV vaccines, US influenza vaccine pricing and immunisation rates, the effect of the May 2026 US executive order on the childhood schedule, Heplisav-B growth after the Dynavax acquisition, and the status of Sanofi's ICC arbitration with AstraZeneca over Beyfortus?

Request: OWN-6

Question: What are the terms, timing and accounting of Sanofi's transfer of 20 mature medicines and three plants to Cheplapharm (September 2026) and its sale of Medley to EMS (March 2026), including the closing dates, the 2025 and 2026 sales and margins of the transferred products, the cash Sanofi receives at closing, the deferred inventory payments, the preferred dividends and earn-outs from 2028 (Deutsche Bank, 18 September 2026), and how Sanofi will account for its 26.4% Cheplapharm stake and its 48.2% Opella stake in business EPS?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 23 November 2026, 23:58 to 00:22 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Sanofi (SAN-FR): Third and fourth sets of questions

The predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months; and how far our assessment is already reflected in company guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative.

Written 23 November 2026 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. "The first set" and "the second set" are the earlier Analysts' answers. Business operating income (BOI) is Sanofi's adjusted operating profit; business EPS is the adjusted earnings per share that sell-side consensus tracks. "Gross-to-net releases" are reversals of rebate accruals made in earlier periods, which add to reported sales.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Before you start: seven things that change how the earlier sets and the model should be read

  1. The forecasts I stand behind. Business EPS on the company's own share count: EUR 9.05 (FY2026), 9.61 (FY2027), 10.38 (FY2028), 11.06 (FY2029) and 11.45 (FY2030), including the second set's assumed EUR 10bn acquisition in mid-2027; without the acquisition about 9.74, 10.48, 10.99 and 11.23. These are the second set's figures less about EUR 0.07 a share from FY2028, because I have removed the contingent Cheplapharm preferred dividends (D-06). I changed three forecast inputs in all (listed after the group judgement on the third set); none changes FY2026 or FY2027 earnings.
  2. The model's EPS is 1.1% to 1.5% too low in every year, for a mechanical reason (the share count), not because of a different view. Read the model summary's "Viewforth against consensus" EPS line (-0.6%, -2.1%, -2.4%, -3.0%) as roughly +0.6%, -0.9%, -1.9% and -2.6% against the FactSet median once the share count and my input change are applied. Its revenue line (-4.4% to -5.1%) is also a basis effect (company net sales against the brokers' revenue, which includes about EUR 2bn of other revenues); like for like our revenue is 0.3% to 1.4% below consensus for FY2026 to FY2028. Its EBITDA line is not comparable at all (definitions differ between brokers).
  3. Do not take three things from the model summary: its half-year profit profile, its net debt, and the dividend part of its 18-month return. Each is distorted by the way reported and forecast periods are joined (explained in the next section). Annual revenue, EBITDA, net income and the earnings and multiple parts of the return are sound.
  4. "Consensus" for FY2027 EPS is two numbers, and the third number quoted by brokers is only an older date. FactSet median EUR 9.70, FactSet mean EUR 9.50 (19 brokers). The "company-compiled consensus" of EUR 9.16 to 9.18 cited by Rothschild & Co Redburn (4 November 2026) and Morgan Stanley (3 November 2026) is not a different definition: FactSet's own mean one month earlier was about EUR 9.13 (EUR 9.50 after a 4.1% rise in one month), so the company-compiled figure is simply the survey taken before the third-quarter results. I disagree with the second set's suggestion (P-06) that a cautious January guide could clear a bar near EUR 9.2; by January the company-compiled consensus will have been re-surveyed near EUR 9.5.
  5. Gross-to-net releases are a recurring feature, not a 2026 event. Sanofi's annual report shows prior-period rebate releases of EUR 155m (2023), EUR 282m (2024) and EUR 667m (2025); GSK's and AstraZeneca's recur every year. Both earlier sets treated the 2026 releases (about EUR 0.45bn to 0.5bn identified by brokers in the second and third quarters) as non-recurring and cleaned them out of the base. That is the prudent treatment and I keep it in the forecasts, but it is a source of upside skew, and it is the one dynamic I found that the sell-side narrative does not hold (I-03).
  6. Four events are newer than the earlier sets' evidence. (a) Bloomberg reported on 19 November 2026 that Sanofi is considering a takeover of Insmed (market value about USD 27bn; about EUR 31bn at a 30% premium per Jefferies; net debt to EBITDA about 2.3 to 2.5 times per Redburn and Jefferies); the shares fell 2.9% that day (EUR 86.25 to EUR 83.79). (b) The strategy update is now dated early December 2026 and has not yet happened; the AlphaSense report commissioned to describe it could not. (c) Sanofi terminated its oral STAT6 degrader from Nurix two months after dosing began, so the first set's statement that Sanofi is "partly hedged" against oral competitors no longer holds. (d) The November 2026 US midterm elections produced divided government (Jefferies, 11 and 16 November 2026): more oversight of drug pricing, little new legislation.
  7. Limits of the evidence. The AlphaSense report on the narrative found no investor commentary (social media, investor letters, positioning surveys) and rests on five brokers' research; everything I say about investors, as distinct from analysts, is inferred from price, short interest and ratings. The AlphaSense reports also quote the Regeneron reimbursement effect in three inconsistent ways (EUR 0.3bn and 0.8bn; EUR 0.6bn; EUR 1.1bn); Regeneron's own accounts settle it (USD 253m to 268m a quarter deducted until the second quarter of 2026, nothing since), and on every reading the 2027 loss against 2026 is about EUR 0.5bn, all in the first half, as the second set modelled.

The Financial Analyst's model against the second set's cross-check: each gap explained

Earnings per share (model EUR 8.95, 9.50, 10.32, 11.01, 11.35; second set EUR 9.05, 9.61, 10.45, 11.13, 11.52). Net income is the same in both (FY2027: EUR 11,569m in the model, EUR 11,550m in the second set). The whole gap is the number of shares: the model divides by about 1,216m to 1,220m (FactSet's diluted snapshot of 1,212.6m plus the input of 4m new shares a year); the second set divides by 1,201m to 1,203m. The second set is right, because consensus is business EPS as the company reports it, and the company's count is 1,199m (third-quarter 2026 business net income of EUR 4,255m divided by reported business EPS of EUR 3.55). On my arithmetic the difference is the roughly 12m shares bought back for EUR 1bn between January and April 2026, which FactSet's snapshot has not removed (FactSet's 1,207.5m at end-2025, less 12m, plus about 4m issued to employees, is 1,199.5m). This is a wrong input for the model's purpose, so I changed it: the FY2026 change in the share count becomes -11 instead of +4, which brings the model to about 1,201m to 1,207m shares and its EPS to within 0.5% of the figures in point 1 above.

Free cash flow (model EUR 7,781m, 8,764m, 9,657m, 11,257m, 11,821m; second set EUR 7,450m, 8,550m, 9,450m, 11,000m, 11,600m; the model is 2% to 4% higher). The model's figure is EBITDA less tax at the full business rate, net financial items, capital spending, lease payments and the change in working capital (FY2027: 16,753 - 2,983 - 600 - 3,500 - 310 - 596 = 8,764, exactly its figure). The second set's figure also deducts restructuring cash (EUR 0.7bn to 0.9bn a year) and adds back the tax saved on amortisation and restructuring (about EUR 0.6bn a year), neither of which the model has a line for. The two omissions nearly offset. No input is wrong and I changed nothing. For the memo, the second set's figure is the better measure of cash available to shareholders, because restructuring has been charged in each of the last eight years.

The FY2026 phasing flag (half-year margins moved by 7.9 points). The model holds first-half 2026 EBITDA of EUR 8,972m (40.6% of sales) on FactSet's "operating" definition, which adds back about EUR 2.1bn of amortisation and impairment of acquired intangibles; on the second set's definition the first half was about EUR 6,875m (31.1%). To keep the year at 32.3% the model cut the second half of 2026 from 33.3% to 25.4%. The year is right; the halves are not. Read literally, the model shows EBITDA falling 19% from the first half of 2026 to the first half of 2027 (EUR 8,972m to about EUR 7,270m); the true like-for-like movement is a rise of about 5% in BOI. No input is wrong. Use the second set's half-year figures (first-half 2027 BOI about +5%, business EPS about +3% to +4%), never the model's.

Net debt (model EUR 20.7bn at end-2026 and EUR 28.6bn at end-2027; second set EUR 11.7bn and EUR 19.4bn on the company's definition). From FY2027 onward the yearly changes agree to within EUR 0.2bn, so the EUR 9bn difference is a level shift created entirely in the model's FY2026. It breaks down as follows, on my reading of the summary's own figures: about EUR 1.7bn is definition (FactSet includes leases); about EUR 1.5bn is the Dynavax purchase counted twice, which the summary's data note itself identifies (the cash left in February 2026 and is already in the reported June 2026 balance, but FactSet files it as a financial investment, so the model charges the second set's full-year acquisition figure again in the forecast half); about EUR 1.6bn is other first-half balance movements that FactSet's cash-flow lines do not explain; and about EUR 4.6bn is an outflow in the model's own FY2026 cash flow beyond anything in the inputs (free cash flow of EUR 7,781m less the model's stated cash flow of EUR -4,895m leaves EUR 12.7bn of deductions, against EUR 8.1bn of dividend, buyback and acquisitions in the inputs). That last amount is the size of one year's dividend. The model demonstrably pays each year's dividend inside that year (FY2027: EUR 5.54bn deducted, which is EUR 4.55 times 1,218m shares), whereas Sanofi pays a year's dividend the following May; at the join between reported and forecast periods this appears to charge a second dividend in 2026 on top of the EUR 4.9bn actually paid in May 2026. I corrected the Dynavax double count through the input (FY2026 acquisitions less disposals becomes -630 instead of -2,150). The rest needs the Financial Analyst. Nothing here touches earnings, because interest is an input. It matters for the memo because the model's end-2027 figure implies about 1.7 times net debt to EBITDA, which would wrongly suggest that even a EUR 10bn acquisition breaks management's guide of about 1 time and endangers the AA rating; the second set's 1.16 times is the figure to use.

The dividend part of the 18-month return (+10.6%). For the same reason, the model credits two dividends (EUR 4.33 and EUR 4.55) by 31 December 2027. A holder from today receives one by then (EUR 4.33 in May 2027; the dividend for 2025 was paid in May 2026), which is 5.2%. The 18-month total return at the guideline multiple is therefore about 84%, not 89%. The three-year column (to 30 June 2029) is unaffected, because three payments do fall before that date.

The Financial Analyst should check two things in the model for Sanofi: first, whether the dividend for FY2026 is being deducted in the forecast half-year of 2026 although the cash dividend of that calendar year was paid in May and is already in the reported June 2026 net debt, and whether the 18-month return should therefore carry one dividend and not two; second, whether the opening share count can be set to the company's count for business EPS (about 1,199m), in which case my changed FY2026 share-count input of -11 should revert to the second set's 4.

Co-movement of SAN-FR with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-23 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Sanofi SA (SAN-FR, Euronext Paris), a global biopharmaceutical company reporting a single Biopharma segment since deconsolidating its consumer health business Opella in April 2025, with franchises in immunology (Dupixent, co-developed and profit-shared with Regeneron), rare diseases and haemophilia (ALTUVIIIO, Ayvakit, Qfitlia, Rezurock), neurology, oncology (Sarclisa), vaccines (influenza, paediatric combinations, Beyfortus, Heplisav-B) and declining general medicines (Lantus, Toujeo, Plavix; Lovenox outside the US being transferred to Cheplapharm in 2027).

Request: OWN-1

Question: What did Sanofi SA announce at its strategy update in early December 2026 under chief executive Belén Garijo (mid-term financial targets for sales, operating margin, business EPS and free cash flow; acquisition criteria and return hurdles; its guide of net debt of about one time EBITDA after acquisitions and its AA credit-rating policy; any share buyback; R&D priorities and spending), and what has been announced or reported since 19 November 2026 about an acquisition by Sanofi of Insmed (first reported by Bloomberg on 19 November 2026) or of any other company, including the price, the premium, the financing, the rating agencies' responses and the effect on business EPS and on net debt to EBITDA that named brokers calculate; how did Sanofi's share price, its brokers' estimates, ratings and price targets change in response to each announcement; and what were the results of, and the reaction to, Kymera Therapeutics' Phase 2b BROADEN2 trial of the oral STAT6 degrader KT-621 in atopic dermatitis (expected around year-end 2026), including what brokers and dermatologists say it means for Sanofi's Dupixent and for rebate levels in the 2028 US formulary round?

Request: OWN-2

Question: At what forward price-to-earnings multiples have large pharmaceutical companies traded in each of the five years before and the three years after the loss of exclusivity of a product that supplied a third or more of their sales (for example AbbVie with Humira, Pfizer with Lipitor, Bristol Myers Squibb with Revlimid and Eliquis, AstraZeneca before 2012, Roche before 2019, Merck & Co with Keytruda, Novartis with Entresto, GSK with dolutegravir), which events moved those multiples up or down (successor launches, large acquisitions, share buybacks, new mid-term targets, patent settlements, guidance upgrades), how large and how fast were the re-ratings and de-ratings, and how do these precedents compare with Sanofi SA, whose 24-month-forward price-to-earnings multiple was about 8.2 times on 20 November 2026 on FactSet consensus, near the bottom of its ten-year range, ahead of the March 2031 expiry of the US composition-of-matter patent on Dupixent; which valuation multiples and methods do named brokers use to set their Sanofi price targets, what discount to European large-cap pharmaceutical peers do they consider justified and why, and what do they say would have to happen for that discount to narrow?

Request: OWN-3

Question: How do investors, as distinct from sell-side analysts, currently discuss Sanofi SA: what is said in fund managers' letters and interviews, in buy-side surveys published by brokers, and in commentary on X (formerly Twitter) and other investor forums since October 2026 about the reported approach to Insmed, the Dupixent patent expiry in 2031, the 2027 earnings outlook and the new management; what do positioning data show (short interest, options activity, changes in the holdings of the largest shareholders including L'Oréal, and fund flows into European pharmaceutical shares), and how do the same measures of sentiment, estimate revisions and share-price momentum stand for GSK, AstraZeneca, Novartis and Roche; and what do brokers and the company-compiled consensus expect for Sanofi's fourth-quarter 2026 results and for the 2027 guidance it will give on 28 January 2027 (sales and business EPS growth at constant exchange rates, the currency effect, the phasing between the first and second half of 2027), including the date and the level of the most recent company-compiled consensus for 2026, 2027 and 2028 business EPS and what brokers assume for US gross-to-net adjustments in the fourth quarter of 2026 and in 2027?
